# Goemon 64 Tools — Development Progress

### 2026-11-15 — Fourth cross-region matching-C function; USA 332-byte full-ROM proof

- **New native C:** Reconstructed the 132-byte alternate character-state reset at USA `func_801DD654_599564` / JP `func_801E2A04_59E9D4`. The argument ABI is `u16`, and source-order of object byte `+0x30` mask and `+0xCC=0xBB` assignment is required for exact IDO register/codegen match.
- **Isolated original-address check:** The 12-byte reader, 40-byte wrapper, 148-byte synchroniser, and 132-byte alternate reset compile/link using native IDO 5.3 to **332/332 byte-for-byte exact** in both USA and Japan (8 exact original-address candidates). Verified against authentic 32 MiB decompressed ROM images.
- **Full USA-ROM proof:** Staged all four functions into isolated pinned `file_11` source with the 180-byte selector still in assembly, using six original-sized C/ASM chunks with relocation-preserving ELF section trimming and local 4-byte link subalignment. Complete 33,554,432-byte rebuild compared byte-for-byte with the original USA reference: SHA-1 `6ea0ed71032ce08fc2745f412d84936382197494`, exact. The clean upstream checkout was reused after its independently verified baseline and 3-function rebuild.
- **Relocation detail:** Original and 4-function `file_11` ELF relocation sections each contain 8,825 entries and have exactly identical `(site, type, target, symbol)` multisets; **three entries are reordered**, so raw order equality is not claimed.
- **Tests:** 14 ROM-free Python unit tests pass; original full USA ROM equality and eight IDO original-address function comparisons pass.
- **Scope:** Updated the region-aware native probe, optional C source, scratch-only full-ROM assembly adapter, tests, proof metadata, and `docs/USA_JAPAN_CHARACTER_SWITCH_MATCHING_2026-11-15.md`. No ROMs or original code bytes are committed.
- **Limitations/next:** Research-verified **four-function/332-byte full-USA-ROM promotion candidate**; unchanged central `decomp/manifest.json`, `decomp.verify` and production matching-C total (160). Japanese full-ROM build remains blocked by upstream hard-coded USA `GLOBAL_ASM` path usage. Next formal gate: integrate mixed C/ASM `file_11` into the central verifier, then rerun its exact 32 MiB G2 and inventory before promotion; target remaining 180-byte selector separately.

### 2026-11-15 — USA + JP character switch: six exact IDO candidates, USA full-ROM and relocations exact

- **Original code mapping:** Pinpointed USA selected-character u32 `0x8015C5DC` versus JP `0x80161DCC`, four 32-bit availability words at USA `0x8015C69C` / JP `0x80161E8C`, and the 180-byte character selector plus 148-byte synchroniser in both `file_11` overlays. These regional offsets are not a universal address delta.
- **Isolated matching C:** Three previously assembly-owned USA functions—12-byte `func_801DD500_599410`, 40-byte `func_801DD4D8_5993E8`, and 148-byte `func_801DD5C0_5994D0`—now compile under original IDO 5.3 -O2 -mips2 and link to the exact original **200/200 bytes** at native addresses. The corresponding JP functions also match their original **200/200 bytes** after region-specific relocation. The synchroniser's `u16` argument type is necessary for matching register allocation.
- **Full USA build proof:** Independently reproduced an exact 33,554,432-byte USA ROM rebuild using pinned upstream Splat, the 200 C bytes and the untouched 180-byte selector assembly. Both original and rebuilt USA SHA-1 are `6ea0ed71032ce08fc2745f412d84936382197494`. Original versus candidate `file_11` ELF relocation lists are **8,825/8,825 identical** in their complete ordered site/type/target/symbol tuples.
- **Reproducibility:** Added research-only regional C, isolated compiler/relocation probe, scratch-USA full-ROM integration script, ELF32 BE MIPS relocation-preserving section-trim helper, regression tests and dated report `docs/USA_JAPAN_CHARACTER_SWITCH_MATCHING_2026-11-15.md`; proof metadata excludes game bytes. The full-ROM rebuild was independently repeated from a fresh upstream ZIP extraction.
- **Tests:** 14 ROM-free Python tests passed; six independent original-address native IDO comparisons passed; clean full USA-ROM equality and `file_11` relocation identity passed. Original Japanese full-ROM rebuild remains blocked by upstream hardcoded USA `GLOBAL_ASM` references.
- **Limits/next:** This is a research-verified production promotion candidate, **not yet registered in the central `decomp.verify` manifest or published matching-C count**. Integrate a mixed C/ASM `file_11` adapter into the central verifier and require fresh G2/relocation and inventory proof before incrementing counts; the 180-byte selector and 132-byte alternate reset remain in ASM.

### 2026-11-15 — Confirmed Japanese character-switch overlay and full MIPS boundaries

- **Original Japanese ROM:** Normalised Japan-0 ROM checksum-verified; 32 MiB decompressed image reproduced SHA-1 `6f8745fecc3a5e7d4918c7a73353eb0726116ded`. The supplied pinned Splat environment extracted original Japanese assembly successfully.
- **18-word trace identification:** Original-code overlay probe matched all 18 Project64 MIPS instructions in `file_11` (0/18 in competing `file_13` and `file_14`). Original Japanese ROM offsets for caller instruction `0x801E2944` and callee entry `0x801E2970`: `0x59E914` and `0x59E940`.
- **Full functions:** Generated assembly identifies a 180-byte character-cycle selector `func_801E28BC_59E88C`, 148-byte character reset `func_801E2970_59E940`, related 132-byte alternate reset `func_801E2A04_59E9D4`, and smaller neighbouring wrappers. The selector checks four slots at `0x80161E8C+4*i`; the reset clears pointed-to halfword `+0x86` and dispatches an engine callback. Detailed meanings/limitations in `docs/JAPAN_CHARACTER_SWITCH_DECOMP_2026-11-15.md`.
- **Tests:** Real Japan-0 SHA-1 check and original 18-word code probe passed. All six Python synthetic tests passed; C research model compiled and ran with strict GCC C11 warnings and ASan/UBSan.

### 2026-11-14 — Player-relative enemy pass-through control

- **Changed:** Added an experimental enemy-body pass-through checkbox in Value freezes, reusing the existing 10 ms worker. Every poll resolves the live pointer at `0x801FC604` and enforces `u8 = 6` at task `+0x30` rather than retaining a fixed actor address. The toggle stops writes when disabled and clears on disconnect, read-only, incompatible session or worker failure.
- **Why:** User gameplay experiment showed state `6` allows passing through enemies; forcing a fixed address cannot survive task relocation and the engine frequently rewrites state `3`.
- **Safety:** Revalidates ordinary gameplay and the task pointer immediately before each write, pauses during transitions, avoids conflicting fixed locks, and never assumes protection from damage or terrain collision. No state `3` restore write is forced when disabled.
- **Tests:** Added synthetic relocation, task-pointer race, pause, session/read-only, fixed-lock conflict and Tk control tests. These are committed as regression coverage; real-game behaviour and complete repository test suite have not been exercised from this connector environment.
- **Next:** Validate live enemy-body pass-through, scene-transition follow, and prompt cancellation in the user's Linux recomp. Investigate native collision check before claiming frame-perfect bypass.

### 2026-11-14 — Automatic damage-event capture and single-branch toolkit workflow

- **Changed:** Added a read-only Automatic events tab with a best-effort 25 Hz player-focused RAM recorder, rolling 30-sample pre-hit history, health-loss trigger at 0x8015C5E4, 3.5-second post-hit window, repeated-event candidate ranking, transient-value timelines, JSON evidence export and one-click Live Memory watches.
- **Why:** Brief post-damage enemy-contact immunity is difficult to investigate with manual Value Search or A/B/C snapshots. Continuous short-range collection keeps the game UI usable and avoids broad 8 MiB polls.
- **Safety:** Capture is read-only, bounded to eight hits and the current map/character/task/object pointer context; a load, invalid pointer or process reconnection stops recording. Stops complete before the previous process handle is closed. Captures are not treated as verified collision semantics.
- **Tests:** Five synthetic damage-recorder tests passed in isolated local Python execution, covering repeatability, pointer/loading rejection, context changes, partial captures and bounded recording. A sixth character/pointer-change test is included in the committed test module; full repository GUI/in-game integration and local decomp CI were not run in this connector-backed session.
- **Branch policy:** chatgptweb/decomp is the current consolidated toolkit integration branch. No new feature branch was made. See docs/BRANCH_WORKFLOW.md. The existing right-click freeze, 100 Hz freeze worker and Linux PID improvements are preserved unchanged.
- **Limitations/next:** Sampling is best-effort and does not guarantee capture of sub-frame flags. Real Goemon64Recomp/Project64 collision and temporary invulnerability behaviour remains unverified; reproduce several damage events and inspect candidate timelines before identifying any writable field.

### 2026-11-14 — Wire fast freeze polling to Tk without depending on UI timers

- Context menu freeze/unfreeze, lock renaming and freeze list rendering use thread-safe ValueFreezes APIs.
- The worker stops when the root window is destroyed; connection swaps are synchronized in a follow-up change.
- No automated or live game tests ran in this connector-only environment.


### 2026-11-14 — Thread-safe fast freeze engine at 100 Hz

- **Changed:** added a 10 ms independent background worker for active freeze locks, instead of relying on Tk's slower callback scheduling. Worker reads/writes game RAM only, never touches Tk. It sleeps longer when idle.
- **Efficiency:** compares each locked range first and skips matching values before additional gameplay checks/verified write. Maintains state checks immediately before changed writes, overlap limits, fixed big-endian bytes, readback and failure-stop semantics.
- **Concurrency:** added an RLock around freeze changes, polling and snapshots, with explicit APIs for removal and label changes. Connection swaps must acquire the same guard.
- **Limitation:** rapid or every-frame game overwrites can still become visible between checks; a truly frame-atomic lock requires modification of the game/runtime, not an out-of-process poller.
- **Tests:** added background-correction, stop/remove, session token and interval regression tests; these were authored but not executed against a live game in this connector environment.

### 2026-11-14 — Attach to existing Linux game PID with explicit OS permission support

- **Changed:** the Connection panel supports direct, explicitly selected PID attachment independently of the game launcher. Added a Linux-only permission control that, with explicit warning and polkit authentication, applies a temporary `kernel.yama.ptrace_scope=0` system setting, plus a control restoring the previous scope recorded in the toolkit session. Permission changes run in a worker; the Tk interface remains responsive.
- **Why:** Linux Yama ptrace scope often blocks same-user `process_vm_readv` against an independently launched game, even with a correct PID. PID selection alone cannot override kernel access rules. No permission changes happen automatically when connecting.
- **Guards:** only stock `pkexec` and `sysctl` binaries are executed for the requested setting; no game or toolkit executable runs as root. System-wide same-user ptrace isolation is relaxed only after the user accepts the warning. The toolkit never writes permanent sysctl configuration, and process ownership / Flatpak limits still apply.
- **Tests:** added OS helper unit tests for scope reporting, the authenticated command and invalid value blocking. Live polkit/game tests not executed in this connector environment.
- **Next:** test external PID on Ubuntu, verify game memory scanning and restore original scope after use.

### 2026-11-14 — Mini Ebisumaru halfword gates attacking and switching; prioritise max speed cap

- **Observed:** user reports legitimate miniature `s16 0x8020C8E6=-1` permits walking, jumping and NPC/sign interaction but prevents ordinary attacks and character switching. Forcing `0` while still visibly miniature removes the attack and switching restrictions, and raises toolkit-derived max movement magnitude from ~32 to ~98. The previously observed miniature appearance carried over through character switching only in this forced-zero state.
- **Interpretation:** this halfword likely selects multiple gameplay restrictions rather than only speed or animation state; original guard instructions and speed mechanism remain unverified.
- **Changed:** updated `docs/EBISUMARU_TRANSFORMATION_RUNTIME_FINDINGS_2026-11-14.md` with the tested action matrix, cross-character clarification and explicit highest-priority search for the native maximum movement-speed limit/parameter or equivalent calculation, rather than only a velocity field. Documentation only, no toolkit code changes.
- **Tests:** user-reported game runtime experiments; no new automated tests or independent MIPS source confirmation.
- **Next:** locate miniature/normal max-speed selection and the attack/switch input gates through original MIPS readers of the halfword, distinguishing stored speed caps from constants and calculated limits.

- **Observed:** user measured toolkit-derived velocity magnitude maxima of about 98 for normal size with `s16 0x8020C8E6 = 0`, 32 for miniature with `-1`, and 98 for miniature forcibly set to `0`. The apparent extra speed is genuine observed displacement, not solely visual perspective.

### 2026-11-14 — Separate Mini Ebisumaru halfword from visible size and character-switch state

- **Evidence:** user forced `s16 0x8020C8E6 = -1` while Ebisumaru remained normal; activating the power briefly shrank him, then restored normal size and `0`. Conversely, forcing `0` while miniature briefly grew him, then returned to miniature and `-1`. Forcing `0` while miniature before switching characters made other playable characters appear miniature.
- **Interpretation:** the halfword is not alone sufficient to define visible model scale. The new causal observations suggest separate transformation/visual state and possible shared or reused player properties at character switch; mechanism and exact original instruction paths remain unverified.
- **Changed:** appended a controlled-write matrix, qualifications, and MIPS/character-switch investigation plan to `docs/EBISUMARU_TRANSFORMATION_RUNTIME_FINDINGS_2026-11-14.md`. Documentation-only change; no toolkit memory-write features added.
- **Tests:** user-reported runtime interventions; no new source code tests or independent gameplay reproduction.

### 2026-11-14 — Strong 16-bit Mini Ebisumaru countdown evidence

- **Observed:** user follow-up video shows `0x8020C8E6/E7` = `00/00` normal and `FF/FF` miniature; `u16` reads 0 or 65535 (`s16` 0 or −1). `0x801C159A` reads 52 normal / 28 mini but can switch before the two-byte field; the upper half of `u32 0x8020C8E4` varies independently.
- **Manual-write observation:** user set just E6 to 255 from a normal state; E6 became 254 while E7 became 255 and counted down with borrow. This is precisely the big-endian `0xFF00 → 0xFEFF → 0xFEFE ...` decrement sequence. User reports transformation only works at all-0 or all-255 endpoints.
- **Changed:** added controlled-write evidence, sample timeline, arithmetic, confidence levels and original MIPS follow-up to `docs/EBISUMARU_TRANSFORMATION_RUNTIME_FINDINGS_2026-11-14.md`. Documentation-only change; no toolkit writes or controls added.

- **Limits:** countdown cadence, specific MIPS width, update ownership, and interpretation of downstream `0x801C159A` remain unknown. Do not extrapolate timer duration.
- **Next:** trace read/write/guard paths for the E6 halfword and identify `0x801C159A`'s role.

### 2026-11-13 — G64-045 VM setup callback ABI (RESEARCH_ONLY)

- **Changed:** recorded the observed owner offsets in an F37C-local research layout and updated its host harness for allocator halfword arguments, exact float bits, callback order, slot wrap and untouched bytes. The pinned allocator reads six stack arguments with `lhu`; F37C reads a word at owner `+0x8C`, reads/writes a byte at `+0xD8`, publishes the returned pointer, then registers two callbacks in order. The full baseline `script_vm.c` unit matched all 10,112 original bytes at VMA `0x8003CFA0`. The one corrected candidate was also 10,112 bytes, but differed in 36 words (115 bytes), all inside F37C. Its first mismatch is `+0x08` / `0x8003F384`: candidate `mtc1 $zero,$f2`, original scale `lwc1 $f2`; the scale LO16 moved from `+0x08` to `+0x0C`, and the allocator call moved from `+0x60` to `+0x5C`. Candidate/source/tool hashes and full details are in `decomp/plans/execution/2026-11-13-g64-045-vm-setup-abi.json`.
- **Tests:** the F37C ASan/UBSan host harness passed 300/300 synthetic cases, including positive/negative zero, infinities, NaN payloads, random bit patterns, slot wrap, callback order and untouched object/control bytes. A positive-zero-to-scale mutant failed at the expected float-bit assertion. The existing broader VM host harness passed under ASan/UBSan. An initial local-CI run caught a stale `script_vm.h` source fingerprint after a temporary shared-header edit; that edit was reverted and the layout remains candidate-local. The final `python3 -m decomp.local_ci` run passed: 162 decomp Python tests, 92 verifier/ledger tests, task/evidence validation, and the display-emitter ASan/UBSan fixture.
- **Limitations/next:** native G1 failed and no second trial was warranted because the predicted register-order discriminator failed and no measured first-mismatch improvement was available. Full-ROM G2, inventory and in-game verification were not run. Stored proof remains 153 matching-C functions / 11,800 bytes and 7,376 inventory entries. Continue with G64-046; keep F37C `GLOBAL_ASM`.

IDO SHA-256 `76d796c9591c9f5504949f85b29c32a6039a663791afe2f36f34ca18425b64d0`; GNU MIPS ld SHA-256 `665c22b6b7c6543a9cdd9373c0e6f6a575db3ece870b133c6dae367706e3848b`.

Inventory passed: 163 C, 7,213 ASM, 7,376 entries, unchanged entry identity. `combined.json` SHA-256 `328be129fe1c39aa31e41a7b37472741a7b0ac968d67a3f17f7489e86ff4ed14`; `inventory.json` SHA-256 `16497366ac28e7aa5cb9d905985ae980acb09d97c97ed832f414bf6a31c6c943`. ## 2026-11-13 — G64-073 file-table mapper trace (RESEARCH_ONLY)

- **Reason and branch:** continued Architect v2 after G64-072 in the existing fresh worktree. Local and remote `chatgptweb/decomp` both began at `3bb5c9408e275defff70d458eab4a35477336301`; the initial worktree was clean.
- **Original evidence:** pinned `mnsg` commit `7e2029c5c7beca705e7ded2c8fa85a5a21d1c3ee` places `func_80001EB0_2AB0` at VMA `0x80001EB0`, ROM offset `0x2AB0`, size 312 bytes / 78 words, SHA-256 `8361ccfbf9f476c692c4b0afdd2a35740f47f05b5042bc7c43c952694a792599`. All 78 words match the private decompressed USA ROM. It calls `80001DF4` as a gate, then reads two words from an eight-byte-stride table row and maps pages through the inclusive `(word1-word0)>>13` count. It skips slots that already equal the destination and otherwise updates the slot before calling `osMapTLB` with six observed arguments. Both direct callers ignore the zero return. The constant caller passes index 60 and therefore selects row 59; a second caller passes a task halfword. The table has no declared extent or observed bounds check.
- **Changes:** added four G64-073 scenarios to the portable C reference in `decomp/test_file_table.c`, including the index-60 row calculation, existing-map skip, repeated request and gate-zero paths. The host-only build now uses fixed-width standard types under `GOEMON_HOST_TEST`. Added [`decomp/plans/execution/2026-11-13-g64-073-file-table-mapper.json`](decomp/plans/execution/2026-11-13-g64-073-file-table-mapper.json); marked G64-073 `RESEARCH_ONLY` and updated phase, queue, strategy, continuation and plan handoffs. Production `file_table.c`, manifest, stored proof and inventory are unchanged.
- **Native experiments:** two pinned IDO 5.3 candidates were linked at `0x80001EB0`. The full-width signature produced 304 bytes (3/78 exact word positions); the caller-declared `unsigned short`/`int` signature produced 320 bytes (11/78). Both first differed at `+0x04`, where the original begins materializing its mask and IDO instead schedules a saved-register store. Each link resolved all six helper, table, pointer-global and `osMapTLB` relocations. The second type hypothesis did not move the first mismatch, so the native search stopped and `GLOBAL_ASM` remains.
- **Tests:** the SHA-verified ROM comparison passed 78/78 words; GCC 15.2 ASan/UBSan host reference cases passed, and an exclusive-end loop mutant failed at the inclusive-page assertion. `python3 -m unittest discover -s decomp -p 'test_*.py' -q` passed **157/157**; `python3 -m unittest tests.test_decomp_verify -q` passed **17/17**; task-context/skill tests passed **17/17**; `python3 -m decomp.skill_context --validate`, the G64-074 task brief, JSON parsing and `git diff --check` passed. Local CI was attempted but unavailable because this branch has no `.github/workflows` directory (exit 1). Complete-ROM G2 and inventory are `NOT_RUN` because no isolated candidate matched.
- **Limitations:** the global table's total extent and the two field meanings remain unknown; the function has no independent count/bounds check. No source-unit exact build, in-game verification, new matching-C function or byte claim was made. The stored proof remains 148 C / 11,020 bytes.
- **Next:** continue with G64-074, characterizing object list `80036308`; keep `func_80001EB0_2AB0` as `GLOBAL_ASM`.

## 2026-11-13 — G64-072 file-table helper trace (RESEARCH_ONLY)

All 24 words match the private decompressed USA ROM. The helper returns zero for input zero; otherwise it scans four-byte records, compares the full unsigned 32-bit input against each unsigned 16-bit boundary, returns byte `+3` at the first boundary strictly greater than the input, and returns `0xFF` for an empty table or the zero-boundary sentinel. All three stopped at the 96-byte target's three-variant limit. The third candidate passed a private GCC 15.2 ASan/UBSan harness for 19 boundary/sentinel inputs; host behavior is not native proof.

Complete-ROM G2 and inventory are `NOT_RUN` because no source-unit-exact candidate was found.
- **Limitations:** the global table's total extent and field meaning remain unknown; this helper relies on valid contents and the zero-boundary sentinel. No in-game verification occurred. No new matching-C function or bytes are claimed.

Inspected the different VM resource lookup `func_8003F038_3FC38` at original VMA `0x8003F038`, ROM `0x3FC38`, **196 bytes / 49 instructions**. The retail ROM SHA-1 remains `df8083a54296b8c151917c5333e1c85f014a2a66`.
- Confirmed three independent control-flow features in original MIPS: `arg==0x100` returns zero without a table lookup; signed division by two rounds toward zero before selecting a signed halfword from `D_80077894`; first resource lookup may trigger `func_80013B14` recovery using the low unsigned halfword before a second unconditional lookup. Second failure returns zero; for half-indices below four, its successful value receives a wrapping signed-halved `arg<<11` offset.
- Added **research-only** `decomp/candidates/vm_resource_halfword_probe.c` implementing these paths. GCC host code explicitly performs unsigned wrapping addition, avoiding signed-overflow undefined behaviour; an isolated test-only table pointer permits negative half-index scenarios without OOB accesses. The target IDO 5.3 `-O2 -mips2 -G 0 -non_shared` source assembled successfully under pinned GNU MIPS Binutils `2.45.90.20260125` with `-G 0 -mips2`. **Compiled function 204 bytes, original 196 bytes**; 3/49 relocation-masked positional words (2/44 non-relocation words) match. Tested alternate signed-half shift/rounding C shapes at 224, 252 and 260 bytes; all remain worse or nonmatching. This is **not** a C promotion.
- **Executed test:** `gcc -std=c11 -Wall -Wextra -Werror -O1 -g -fno-pie -no-pie -fsanitize=address,undefined -fno-sanitize-recover=all` compiled `decomp/test_vm_resource_halfword_probe.c`; **3,000/3,000 deterministic synthetic host scenarios passed**, including negative signed indices, 0x100 early return, resource retry, low-half recovery argument, second-call failure and wraparound arithmetic. Independent explicit arithmetic/reference logic checked the outputs. These are mock callbacks and synthetic test-table values, **not** original-MIPS emulation or in-game testing.
- Added `decomp/test_vm_resource_original.py` as a fail-closed original-assembly guard: **16/16 semantic/branch-delay-slot instructions passed** against privately recovered source, plus **five passing negative/parser tests** and `py_compile`. The SHA-256 digest of original 196-byte instruction extent is `7a258b9eae36120d3971f1997070c2a988fb9636cbd4c96f1f52ea888e7b9719`. The 204-byte isolated target function SHA-256 is `d8b87022a4bad2099c9f9396f4d906a754c05032be034171951e90c2cdb22332`. These diagnostic hashes and original assembly stay unpromoted; no private assets, ROM, generated assembly or toolchains were committed.
- **Limits:** No new full-ROM integration, uninterrupted formal `decomp.verify`, or game execution. Production `script_vm.c`, manifest and `results/combined.json`/`inventory.json` remain unchanged. **142 verified C / 10,652 MIPS bytes, 155 combined C entries and 7,221 remaining ASM** remain authoritative.

## 2026-11-09 - corrected VM loader host-resource-ID endianness (UNPROMOTED)

- Inspected original USA loader assembly at `0x8003D4CC`: `lhu $a0, +2($s1)` reads the low 16 bits of the big-endian 32-bit resource ID. Identified a **false-positive host test**: the existing union's `.half.lower` at offset +2 gives the **high** 16 bits on little-endian x86, and the existing assertion compared against that wrong value.
- Updated the research-only VM loader with a host-only `GOEMON_DECOMP_HOST_TEST` resource-ID accessor that obtains the numeric low 16 bits. Preserved the target MIPS `lhu +2` union access under normal IDO compilation. Updated the mock-game test to define the host flag and vary high and low resource-ID halfwords independently over 100 iterations.
- **Executed:** GCC C11 `-Wall -Wextra -Werror -O1 -g -fno-strict-aliasing -fsanitize=undefined -fno-sanitize-recover=undefined` compiled and passed **400/400 deterministic mock-game paths** (100 each no owner, normal load, recovery/retry, clear). The previous source paired with the new assertions failed as intended (assertion `recovered_halfword == (u16)resource_id`, exit 134), confirming that the new check catches the old incorrect model. A padded/little-endian host is not used as evidence of game behaviour outside these synthetic scenarios.
- **Pinned target-code regression:** IDO 5.3 `-O2 -mips2 -G 0 -non_shared` compiled the revised research C; GNU MIPS Binutils `2.45.90.20260125` assembled both old/new variants with `-G 0 -mips2`. Their complete **560-byte .text payloads are byte-identical** (SHA-256 `4c4353a1fbc87c5cf7c5350b60037ecc999f4aa782542f1dfcf6851a5346523d`). The original is **548 bytes** with saved `$s0/$s1`; this revision makes **no instruction-match progress**.
- Additional isolated compiler probes for `register` pointers, volatile pointers/pointees, pointer-casts, declaration position and inverted/goto null-owner branches did not reproduce the saved-register codegen. Volatile pointer qualifiers alone produced a 32-byte frame but still did **not** save `$s0/$s1`; frame size alone is not matching-C evidence.
- **Limits:** no new ROM rebuild, no complete uninterrupted formal `decomp.verify`, no in-game testing, no change to manifest, production VM source, result reports or claimed counts. Remain at **142 exact functions / 10,652 bytes, 155 combined C, 7,221 ASM**.
- **Next:** continue original-register-lifetime and delay-slot analysis; any new loader C must pass original MIPS bytes and complete-ROM identity independently before promotion.

## 2026-11-09 - VM text-loop aliasing/control-flow research: 47→53 comparable instructions (UNPROMOTED)

- Recovered the user's pinned IDO 5.3 compiler from `goemon-matching-rebuild.zip`, verified the original 472-byte text-loop instructions from the user-provided decompressed USA ROM (32 MiB SHA-1 `6ea0ed71032ce08fc2745f412d84936382197494`), restored the compiler-stage executables and extracted pinned MIPS Binutils for disassembly. Reproduced the existing `decomp/candidates/vm_text_loop.c` isolated `-O2 -mips2 -G 0 -non_shared` result: **472-byte symbol; 47/95 original non-relocation words equal; 0x50-byte stack frame** (original 0x48).
- Tested targeted interface and control-flow variations: pointer-only field access, preloaded target values/addresses, terminal-state comparisons, retry branch restructuring, read/write qualifier changes and explicit argument lifetime. Most failed to improve IDO scheduling. The useful change was **one evidence-bound script-word load/store aliasing assumption**: treat the VM work pointer as volatile, explicitly load its script word into `script_arg` just before the step callback, and store the result via the work pointer. This coaxes IDO to load `$a1` from saved `$s1` before `jal` and fill the retry branch delay slot with the correct reload. The isolated function remains **472 bytes**, improves to **53/95 exact comparable instruction words** with 23 relocation positions, and still has the incorrect `0x50` stack frame; it is **not** an exact match.
- Added `decomp/candidates/vm_text_loop_alias_probe.c`, separate from canonical `vm_text_loop.c` and production `script_vm.c`; `decomp/results/vm_text_loop_alias_probe.json` records the diagnostic and exclusions. Added `decomp/test_vm_text_loop_alias_probe.py` to fail closed if the canonical candidate changes or if the alternate accidentally alters unrelated control flow/field widths. **Three structural unit tests passed**, and the exact final research C source compiled with pinned IDO 5.3 to a 472-byte symbol, confirmed **53/95** original-word positions.
- Executed a local original-MIPS-vs-host-C differential interpreter over **10,000 synthetic scenarios** (5,000 each with deterministic seeds `0x1d8`, `0xbeef`), using the original instructions from the hash-verified local ROM. The alternate host C was compiled with GCC C11 `-Wall -Wextra -Werror -O1 -fsanitize=undefined -fno-sanitize-recover=undefined`; all scenarios passed, including callback-mutated state/bounds, controller +2/+4 halfwords, both counters, early returns, null scripts, 3–6 terminal states, repeated input. The synthetic harness uses mock game callbacks and **is not in-game verification**.
- **Limits / next:** The new variant is **research-only** and retains many incorrect instructions; no full-ROM link or uninterrupted `decomp.verify` was attempted for it. Authoritative promoted totals remain **142 matching C / 10,652 bytes; 155 combined C; 7,221 ASM**. Focus on candidate `0x7C–0x8C` scheduler/store ordering and `0x50` vs `0x48` frame without accepting new sizes/relocations as progress by themselves. Run an uninterrupted 142-function formal verifier when the complete pinned build environment is restored.

## 2026-11-09 - permanent curated binutils environment in formal verifier (build plumbing; unpromoted)

- Fixed the reproducibility gap left by the earlier isolated linker recovery: `prepare_mips_binutils.py` previously required manually evaluating shell exports, while `decomp.verify` launched all `make` and MIPS subprocesses without that environment. `decomp.verify` now accepts `--binutils-root` or `GOEMON_MIPS_BINUTILS_ROOT`; when specified it creates a private five-DSO runtime under the fresh workdir, validates the **pinned** assembler, linker, objcopy, objdump and nm, then explicitly passes a curated child-process environment to every build subprocess and tool lookup. - **Executed locally:** nine focused Python tests passed (five existing curated-runtime cases + four new verifier wiring tests), `py_compile` passed on all modified Python files, and `decomp.verify --help` displayed the new flag. A **real extracted user-provided** MIPS Binutils `2.45.90.20260125` archive passed version probes for all **five** tools and built a synthetic two-instruction MIPS function through `as → ld → objdump` with the curated environment, with zero parent-environment changes and no `libc.so.6` staged. ## Current status — 2026-11-01

**Baseline:** v0.9.9 source recovered from the original release ZIP. The deleted conversation was reconstructed separately from 102 screenshots; screenshots and personal conversation transcript are not committed to this public repository.

**Verified locally (source audit, 2026-11-01):**
- Python `pytest -q`: **84 passed, 5 subtests passed**.
- Python compilation: `python -m compileall -q autosplitter practice tests` passed.
- Portable C checkpoint/watch unit tests compiled with `cc -std=c11 -Wall -Wextra -Werror` and passed in a simulated environment.
- **Not verified:** launching the Windows GUI and observing scene/character operations on a running US Goemon64Recomp instance. Automated tests are not evidence of successful in-game warps.

**Working implementation (based on source inspection):**
- Windows-targeted game-memory connection and practice UI.
- LiveSplit autosplitter bridge/engine and an import path for route definitions.
- Read-only scene/transition tracing and native debug-menu observation tools.
- Guarded live N64 guest-memory editing; dangerous fields require explicit additional opt-in.
- Logical checkpoint/character state assistance (not a full N64 savestate).
- Portable C checkpoint/watch prototypes; **not yet installed in a custom N64 ROM**.

**Current blocker:** direct one-click scene warp. The experimental v0.9.7 pending-destination + exit-request write returned to the title screen. v0.9.9 correctly disables this unsafe route. Locate the actual engine scene/door loading routine; do not re-enable direct warp based only on map-ID changes.

**Observed normal door sequence (MACHI 353 → 351):**
- `0x800C7CA0` pending destination changed at ~2715 ms.
- `0x800C7AB2` current map changed at ~3112 ms (~397 ms later).
- `0x800C7ABC` previous map and `0x800C7AB4` stage offset changed with the committed current map.
- This establishes an observed order of values, **not** the invocation or address of a safe scene loader.

**Useful guest-RAM addresses (US build / experimental):**

| Address | Interpretation |
|---|---|
| `0x800C7A94` | Top-level game state; observed debug menu 10 and gameplay 13 |
| `0x800C7AA4` | Debug-menu category / context-dependent gameplay submode |
| `0x800C7AB2` | Current absolute stage/map ID |
| `0x800C7AB4` | Native stage offset |
| `0x800C7ABC` | Previous map ID |
| `0x800C7CA0` | Pending destination |
| `0x800C7A9F` | Suspected exit request; earlier direct write returned to title |
| `0x800C7E60` | Menu-entry pointer, **not** a standalone character ID |
| `0x8015C5DC` | Active gameplay character |

Known debug-menu category bases: SIRO 0, DOUCHU 300, MACHI 350. Additional mappings require validation. These addresses are observational findings, **not** authorisation to perform raw writes.

## Next development steps

1. Trace the native scene loader or door-transition function in the recompilation/reverse-engineered game code; corroborate against a real door transition.
2. Establish a minimal and safe state-preparation + load invocation, first in disposable saves with checks/rollback strategy.
3. Validate the change with a reproducible in-game test, capturing expected versus observed room, player state and failures.
4. Continue work on native N64 ROM menu integration only after the appropriate runtime and build integration are understood.
5. Keep the autosplitter independent from experimental writes and protect competitive-run integrity.

## 2026-11-02 — v0.11.3: Ushiwaka story-event probes, Sasuke battery UI, native pre-load character test

- **Ushiwaka first A/B JSON** `state_probe_20261102_110106_887579.json`: Goemon (character 0), map 371, 11:00:51.517→11:01:04.456 UTC, **before starting minigame vs after starting**. Within the persistent save event flag block, guest `0x8015C60D` changed **`0x20 → 0x30`**, bit **`0x10` set**. Strong *minigame-start/storyline-state* candidate. Other differences are transient/dynamic; no C captured. Needs controlled bit test before marking confirmed.
- **Ushiwaka second A/B JSON** `state_probe_20261102_110210_650667.json`: same character/map, 11:02:05.125→11:02:09.012 UTC, **before vs after blue-fish completion**. Same guest `0x8015C60D` changed **`0x30 → 0x38`**, bit **`0x08` set**. Blue fish quantity `0x8015C723` changed **`0x03 → 0x00`**. Bit 0x08 is a strong blue-fish completion candidate, not yet a confirmed Achilles' Heel grant or final quest completion. No C capture. Item ownership and storyline completion are separately checked in gameplay, per user's manual tests.
- **Sasuke second battery UI:** confirmed observational timing is first-battery bit `0x20`, then post-cutscene `0x40`: `0x8015C609` becomes `0x60` **at the end of** battery 2 cutscene. It is not the cutscene command. Added a clearly visible **read-only** indicator near the top of Unlocks (also already in Live Memory), plus both Ushiwaka candidate flags with explicit unverified labels.
- **Native cursor correction:** user's `0x800C7E60` persists at **128** once native menu has been opened. Original Goemon/Ebisumaru/Sasuke/Yae `0x8007BAD0...BAF4` pointer progression applied only while highlighting entries in the active native menu. The pointer is **not** a robust gameplay character selector and is never written to trigger a warp.
- **No C-Down workaround:** removed experimental scene browser's automatic post-load C-Down presses. Scene reload now optionally stages *only* global character `0x8015C5DC` (u32 0..3) **before** staging top-level game state `0x0C`, allowing the original initial actor/model loader to choose the character if it honours the ID. Source RE names spawn loader `func_801CB5D0` (reads this global) and `func_801CBC40` (initial model load); it also documents area-transition reset writes, so this is an **unverified hypothesis** until observed in-game. Does not modify per-task ID, menu pointer or XYZ. If pre-load staging fails, restores pending-map/character only while safely in source gameplay; after reload it only verifies both loaded global ID and task-local character ID. No automatic fallback or post-load writes on failure. Party-unlocked status not required for the *experimental initial loader*; this is different from normal C-Down party swapping.
- **Tests:** `xvfb-run -a python -m unittest discover -s tests -q` **171 passed** in simulated game RAM / Linux Tk. Covers pre-load write, invalid-character no-write, character-write rollback, no post-load C-Down, wrong-char result and new Unlocks displays. No Windows in-game validation; potential actor mismatch, wrong spawn, crash/softlock. Use disposable saves.
- **Next:** Observe whether the actor/model actually spawns as chosen in US Goemon64Recomp. If it does not, trace `func_80021444` transition reset and the native menu's character-to-spawn commit instead of post-loading swaps.

- **Live user observation:** second Sasuke battery completion changes packed event byte `0x8015C609` from `0x20` to `0x60` **after** the pickup cutscene ends. Thus bit `0x40` is an observed second-cutscene-complete marker, not evidence of the cutscene trigger. Added a read-only decoded Live Memory watch; did not add an unverified event-trigger write.
- **Character scan:** user-repeated native debug cursor sweep (Goemon/Ebisumaru/Sasuke/Yae then reverse) in map 404, menu state 10, 8 stable samples each. `0x800C7E60` four-byte pointer follows `0x8007BAD0 + 0x0C*character` (0/1/2/3). `0x800C7E62`/`63` are overlapping truncated representations, not independent fields. This is the menu-entry pointer, **not an integer character ID** and should not be written outside the original menu. Other 60 hits are lookup patterns without standalone semantic proof.
- **Fix:** Scene browser's character dropdown was completely ignored by experimental 0x0C reload. It is now captured with the destination and, after 3 playable samples, applied through the original game C-Down switch if that character is already unlocked in the party. Verify idle action, map, global character and per-task ID after native model swap. Up to 4 spaced native key presses or manual C-Down fallback; timeout, wrong map and missing party character fail explicitly. Never directly write character ID, task, or menu-pointer addresses. Native menu may load even locked characters; this fallback cannot mimic that.
- **Testing:** automated synthetic-RAM and Tk/Xvfb tests. **Not yet live-tested with a Windows game**, character-specific spawn/coordinates still unresolved.
- **Next:** verify C-Down automatic input on Windows with each target; test whether `0x800C7E60` is writable and menu-owned *inside native debug menu only* before trying to emulate its character-spawn path.

## 2026-11-02 — v0.11.0 cross-area logical checkpoint restore and responsive connection

- **Cross-area checkpoints:** Restore now detects if the live map differs from the checkpoint map. It requests the user-tested experimental stage reload by writing the saved map to `0x800C7CA0` and setting game state `0x800C7A94=0x0C`. It **does not** write saved XYZ, rotation, save data, resources or headers during the reload. A polled state machine waits until the actual loaded map ID equals the checkpoint map, ordinary gameplay (`0x0D`), no transitions/scripts, an idle live player, and at least three stable player-pointer observations after a minimum settling delay; only then does it call the existing guarded logical restore, which writes the checkpoint's exact saved coordinates. It preserves the existing native C-Down character-switch path if the loaded character differs. Same-area restores remain immediate. Cancel, a wrong loaded map, timeout and terminal write errors prevent unsafe follow-on writes; final restore errors are not retried.
- **Responsive Connect:** RAM discovery, process opening and snapshot validation moved to a daemon worker thread. Tk is updated only on its main thread via a polled completion queue, and Connect is disabled during discovery. The UI remains responsive even if scanning takes time, but discovery itself may still be slow.
- **Tests:** `xvfb-run -a python -m unittest discover -s tests -q` with synthetic RDRAM and Tk UI, **161 passing tests** (plus nine discovery-class fixtures from imports in isolated targeted runs). Regression tests for cross-area load delays, script lock, timeout/wrong map, character swapping, terminal failed writes, same-area behaviour and responsiveness while a worker blocks.
- **Limits:** This is an experimental **logical** checkpoint, **not** an emulator savestate. Camera, actor spawns, enemies, animation/physics and active cutscenes cannot be reconstructed. The game may move/snap coordinates after the tool writes them, or its forced reload may softlock. No real Windows game integration run has been performed. A disposable/backed-up save is required for safe experimentation.

## 2026-11-02 — First Sasuke battery CONFIRMED by intervention

User wrote `0x20` to `0x8015C609` before collecting first battery. After reload pickup disappeared and second battery became available. `gamedata+0x01` bit `0x20` is confirmed as first-battery progression. Unlocks sets/clears only this bit, preserving other flags. Old `0x8015C70B` guess removed from UI. Second battery not mapped; no separate second-battery JSON attached in latest turn. Synthetic-memory/UI tests passed.

## 2026-11-02 — v0.10.8 experimental 0x0C stage reload and task-float pulse

- Added opt-in **EXPERIMENT reload** in Scene browser (scene selection and double-click remain read-only). Uses user-observed sequence: write pending destination `0x800C7CA0` as u16, then top-level state `0x800C7A94 = 0x0C`. Never writes old exit flag or fabricates spawn XYZ. Read-only monitoring exports source map/character/position and observed result; reaching target map is **not evidence of correct entrance**.
- Added `PlayerTask +0x68` dynamic f32 live watch and experimental buttons for +4.0, −4.0 and 1.5× scaling (single 4-byte write). No 8-byte overlap; no unproven velocity label. Requires both source hex-edit capability and UNSAFE toggle.
- Updated source app version, Windows batch launcher, research guide and memory map. 147 automated tests and 9 subtests passed under Xvfb, including simulated GUI controls. Windows game connection and actual speed/wrap behavior have **not** been tested.
- Next: compare native-debug-menu spawn coordinates with `0x0C` reload, and record effects of positive/negative task float writes against XYZ motion. Player task base must be re-resolved per load.

## 2026-11-02 — weapon index confirmed; map, magic and player-task false positives refined

- **Equipped weapon:** `0x8015C607` (one byte) is user-confirmed across Goemon, Ebisumaru, Sasuke and Yae. Normal values `0x01`, `0x02`, `0x03` select the three current-character weapons. Manually writing out-of-range values accesses unintended next-character weapon artwork/behaviour: Goemon -> Ebisumaru hammer; Ebisumaru -> Sasuke kunai; Sasuke -> Yae sword; Yae -> what user described as `letter 1`. Exact out-of-bounds dispatch mechanics not proven. Added built-in live watch, showing unusual values as CHECK, and unsafe-edit policy permitting any single byte.
- **Map overlay:** `0x8015C574` *reads* `00` while hidden and `01` while displayed (new live user evidence). However manual writes of any tested value open the map, including `00` which does not close it. Separate read behaviour from write behaviour; never implement an `00=close` button from this evidence. Added observed-state live watch.
- **Rejected magic candidate:** `0x800C7A73` oscillates `00/01` outside magic activation. It is NOT a confirmed magic-active flag; find another source through activation-specific testing.
- **Rejected weapon candidate:** `0x8014C340` oscillates `0x40`/`0x41` and sometimes `0x00` independently. Not a dedicated selected-weapon field.
- **Velocity reassessment:** dynamic task float fields `+0x68`/`+0x70`/`+0x74`/`+0x78`/`+0x7C` (observed at `0x8016A0F8`/`0x8016A100`/`0x8016A104`/`0x8016A108`/`0x8016A10C` for one session) read zero while stationary but fluctuate during NPC conversations, weakening their identification as direct locomotion velocity. User reports maximum byte-readings ~192 (and ~191 on `+0x7C`); these are likely first bytes of IEEE-754 floats, e.g. `0xC0=192` is part of `-2.7127` at `+0x68`, **not a 192-unit velocity maximum**. Watch entire four-byte big-endian f32, and always resolve the task pointer dynamically.
- Automated tests now verify built-in equipped-weapon watch, map observed-state watch and unsafe out-of-range weapon edits. Windows gameplay not reproduced inside test runner.

Watch definitions are stored in `custom_watches.json` in the existing source/user-data directory, not committed to GitHub.
- Added **Watch selected address** to the State discovery comparison screen for quick transfer of candidate addresses to Live memory.
- **New live finding:** the user's controlled manual writes to `0x8015C574` of `0x00`, `0x01` and other values **all caused the map to open**. Writing zero did not close it. Therefore `0x8015C574` is NOT validated as a normal map-visible 0/1 flag; could be a command/refresh trigger or a different state variable with side effects. Preserve candidate status. Do not ship a map-close toggle based on this field.
- Tests: custom watch parsing/persistence/big-endian decoding, GUI add/remove/live refresh/discovery import, mocked RAM only. No Windows live game session available in this environment.

### 2026-11-02 — Project64 connection and fragmented RDRAM discovery

- Changed: Dashboard connection selector for auto/recomp/project64; process selection rejects ambiguous instances instead of choosing the first PID. RAM discovery coalesces adjacent readable memory regions before scanning, preserving gaps and the existing pointer/player/endian validation. Corrected stale GUI version/host labels. Existing Python modules, autosplitter logic and portable C core are retained.
- Why: Project64's live 8 MiB RDRAM buffer was split into regions with different page protections, including small protected-code regions skipped by the previous minimum-region-size filter. The original scanner rejected all candidates despite valid original US addresses.
- Tested: `python -m unittest discover -s tests -q` — 172 passed on Windows, including Tk tests, fragmented-page scans, a signature across chunk boundaries, inaccessible-gap preservation, process ambiguity and read-only GUI worker selection. `git diff --check` passed. Read-only process scan and actual Tk Live memory connection to Project64 3.0.1.5664 succeeded: system pointer, Goemon ID, player/task pointers, map, HP/resources and coordinates decoded at existing guest addresses. User moved Goemon; successive probes changed map 561/XYZ (90, 0, -80) to map 353/XYZ approximately (-143.67, 0, -122.67). GUI showed map 353/Oedo Town South, Goemon, HP 10, Ryo 100 and lives 3. Test process handle was opened without write permissions. No game memory was written by these validation steps.
- Limitations: This verifies the sampled fields on one live US configuration, not every address, ROM revision, quest bit or cross-host write behavior. Project64 warps, checkpoint restores and writes remain untested. Discovery still requires an 8 MiB readable range and a loaded player; 4 MiB support is deferred. Portable C was unchanged and not rebuilt for this connection-only change. Existing executables require rebuilding/restarting to include it.
- Next: Use read-only State discovery on Project64 to collect repeated labelled event captures and compare candidates with existing recomp research; add an evidence registry and repeated-trial filtering before promoting any new flag semantics.

## Mandatory change-log template

```markdown
### YYYY-MM-DD — Short change title
- Changed: ...
- Why: ...
- Tested: exact commands/results; specify if in-game testing was NOT performed
- Remaining: ...
- Next: ...
```

**Commit rule:** Every functional/documentation change must update this file in the same commit. Push the commit to `main` (or push a feature branch and merge it) as part of completing the change; never claim a push succeeded without checking GitHub.
